‫—עֵץ ַחּיִים ִהיא‬The Torah is
a tree of life for those who
attach themselves to it, and
those who uphold it are
happy. Its ways are pleasant
and bring peace, and it
ensures eternal life and a
happy and meaningful life;
indeed, the Torah is vast
and magnificent.

Instant Insight:
The first four
requests we make
from Hashem all
begin with the
phrase “‫י ְִהי ָרצֹון ִמ ִּל ְפנֵי‬
‫ — ָא ִבינּו ֶׁש ַּב ָּׁש ַמיִם‬May it
be the will of our
Father in Heaven,”
but the last one,
“‫ִׂש ָר ֵאל‬
ְ ‫א ֵחינּו ָּכל ֵּבית י‬,”
ַ
does not. Why?
Because when
we view all fellow
Jews as “‫ — ”אחינו‬all
members of one
big family, and
there is unity
among us — that
itself ensures that
Hashem will answer
our tefillos.

97 Shacharis • Hachnasas Sefer Torah

Returning the Torah

‫הכנסת ספר תורה‬
‫ ובנחה יאמר‬/ ‫ לדוד מזמור‬/ ‫ הודו‬/ ‫יהללו‬
:‫מחזירין את ספר התורה למקומו והש”ץ אומר‬

: ֹ‫ש ָ ּגב ׁ ְשמוֹ ְל ַבדּ ו‬
ׂ ְ ִ‫יְ ַה ְלל ּו ֶאת ׁ ֵשם יְ הֹוָ ה ִּכי נ‬
‫ְה ְללּו ֶאת ֵׁשם‬
ַ ‫( — י‬Holding the Sefer Torah, the chazzan calls out:) May the whole world
recognize the Presence of Hashem, and praise Him for all that He does!
:‫והקהל אומרים‬

‫ וַ ָ ּי ֶרם ֶק ֶרן ְל ַע ּמוֹ ְּת ִה ָּלה ְל ָכל ֲח ִס ָידיו‬,‫הוֹ דוֹ ַעל ֶא ֶרץ וְ ׁ ָש ָמיִ ם‬
:‫ש ָר ֵאל ַעם ְקרֹבוֹ ַה ְללוּיָ ּה‬
ׂ ְ ִ‫ִל ְבנֵ י י‬
‫ְׁש ָמיִם‬
ָ ‫( — הֹודֹו ַעל ֶא ֶרץ ו‬And we respond:) (Everyone should realize that) the majesty of
Hashem is far beyond the earth and the Heaven; and that we, Bnei Yisrael, are His
chosen nation and have always remained close to Him.

‫ְל ָדוִ ד ִמזְ מוֹ ר ַליהֹוָ ה ָה ָא ֶרץ ו ְּמלוֹ ָא ּה ֵּת ֵבל‬
‫ ִּכי הוּא ַעל יַ ִּמים יְ ָס ָד ּה וְ ַעל‬:‫וְ י ׁ ְֹש ֵבי ָב ּה‬
‫ ִמי יַ ֲע ֶלה ְב ַהר יְ הֹוָ ה ו ִּמי‬:‫נְ ָהרוֹ ת יְ כוֹ נְ נֶ ָה‬
‫ נְ ִקי ַכ ּ ַפיִ ם ו ַּבר ֵל ָבב‬: ֹ‫יָ קוּם ִּב ְמקוֹ ם ָק ְד ׁשו‬

for the day when all of civilization
will recognize Hashem and live by
these values.

LET’S TAKE ALL THIS HOME!

‫אשרי — ובא לציון — עלינו‬
‫ שיר של יום‬/ ‫ עלינו‬/ ‫ ובא לציון‬/ ‫ למנצח‬/ ‫אשרי‬
We now must wind down our tefillos, sum up the main points, and ensure that
we transition properly from a state of being in the direct Presence of Hashem
and talking to Him back to the activities of our day-to-day life. Our hope is
that thanks to this morning’s tefillah, today we will focus on our mission and
live up to our potential — at least a little more than yesterday!

‫אשרי‬
Why are we saying this again?
• As we pointed out earlier, Ashrei follows the aleph-beis. The significance
is that in all areas of our life (aleph to tav), we need Hashem and His help,
we are supported by Him, and His providence is constant. As we transition
out of tefillah into our regular school day/workday, it is crucial to keep this
in mind.
• Out there in the “real world,” it can be difficult sticking to our values, and
many times we find ourselves in uncomfortable situations. Therefore, we
want to stress to ourselves that “‫ — ”אשרי יושבי ביתך‬Happy are those who,
wherever they are, are living in the house of Hashem (i.e., are living a
meaningful and spiritually growing life), and that true happiness comes
from not compromising our principles under social pressure.
• In Pesukei D’Zimrah, the phrase “‫ ”פותח את ידיך‬is understood as a statement
praising Hashem: He opens His Hand and gives everyone what they need.
Here, as we transition out of tefillah into our workday, it is understood as a
request: Please, Hashem, open Your Hand and give us what we need!
• As we transition to our everyday activities, we must focus on the last line
of “‫ — ויברך כל בשר שם קדשו לעולם ועד‬Everyone (will be so impacted by my words
and actions, that they too) will bless Hashem,” and we must be sure to
conduct ourselves in such a manner wherever we are and whatever we do.
The following is a preface to ‫תהילים קמה‬, which begins below. These words
express that it is a wonderful and joyous opportunity to praise Hashem and
not, chas v’shalom, a burden!

‫ עוֹ ד‬,‫ית ָך‬
ֶ ‫ַא ׁ ְש ֵרי יוֹ ׁ ְש ֵבי ֵב‬
‫ ַא ׁ ְש ֵרי ָה ָעם‬:‫יְ ַה ְללו ָּך ּ ֶס ָלה‬
‫ ַא ׁ ְש ֵרי ָה ָעם ׁ ֶשיְ הֹוָ ה‬, ֹ‫ׁ ֶש ָּכ ָכה לו‬
:‫ֱאל ָֹהיו‬

101 Shacharis • Ashrei

Instant Insight:
There is no ‫ נ׳‬in
Ashrei — why? The
Maharal says that
these pesukim,
starting with the
letters of the
aleph-beis, express
the inherent
meaning (based on
Kabbalah) of those
letters. The inherent
meaning of ‫ נ׳‬is
‫ — נפילה‬a falling. Here
David HaMelech
is describing what
Hashem does, and
Hashem does not
cause our fallings!
Our mistakes are
the result of our
own poor choices!
In the next verse
we are told what
Hashem does
in regard to our
fallings —‫סֹומְך ְיהֹוָה ְל ָכל‬
ֵ
‫ — ַהּנ ְֹפ ִלים‬He helps us
get back up!
Crucial Instant
Insight:
It does not say
that Hashem is
close to all “‫קראהו‬
‫—”באמת‬who
now call to Him
with honesty
and integrity.
Rather, “‫אשר יקראהו‬
‫—”באמת‬who will
call to Him with
honesty, in the
future tense. This
means that if right
now you have
a very “up and
down” relationship
with Hashem, and
you struggle with
being 100 percent
sincere, 100 percent
of the time, and
at times you are a
bit of a hypocrite
as far as what you
promise Hashem
and what you end
up doing—still, as
long as you are
committed to being
“‫—”יקראהו באמת‬your
goal is to be
completely sincere,
honest, and genuine
in your relationship
with Hashem—then
already now He
will listen to Your
requests for help to
get there!

‫ְל ָכל‬
‫סוֹ ֵמ ְך יְ הֹוָ ה‬
‫ וְ זוֹ ֵקף ְל ָכל‬,‫ַה ּנ ְֹפ ִלים‬
:‫ַה ְּכפו ִּפים‬

‫סֹומְך ְיהֹוָה ְל ָכל ַהּנ ְֹפ ִלים‬
ֵ —
(Though Hashem is “busy”

but then by your actions distance yourself from Hashem; (2) in general,
whenever you speak it is with honesty, i.e., you are a “man of your word”
and sincere; and (3) it is clear from your actions that you are sincere in
your requests from Hashem, since you are doing whatever you personally
can do to achieve those goals.)

‫נְקֶוּה ְּלָך‬
ַ ‫— ַעל ֵּכן‬Therefore, we hope that
soon we will see Your power and glory
when You destroy all of society’s false
gods, and all of humanity will recognize
Your Presence, and all the wicked will
submit to You and accept the yoke of
Your Kingdom; and You will rule over
the whole world; soon and forever!
‫ֶא ַמר‬
ֱ ‫ — ְונ‬As the pasuk says: “Then
Hashem will be the King over the
entire earth; on that day Hashem will
be One (everyone will realize there is
no other power but Hashem), and His
Name will be One (everyone will realize
that all that has happened since creation
is all good, and all came from Hashem as
part of one unified plan).”

107 Shacharis • Shir Shel Yom

‫שיר של יום‬
Each Day Is Unique and Gets Us Closer to Shabbos — and Closer to Mashiach!
Each day, as part of the avodah in the Beis Hamikdash, the Leviim sang a
different “song” (chapter of Tehillim) that (according to the Gemara) represents
that day’s aspect of Creation. In the hope that soon the Beis Hamikdash will
be rebuilt, we also recite songs every day. By counting the days in reference to
Shabbos (‫)היום יום … בשבת‬, we fulfill the mitzvah to remember the Shabbos every
day. In addition, this ties our daily successes to Shabbos, rather than to the
planets (which is the source of the non-Jewish names of each day). According
to the Zohar, each chapter connects to a different millennium of the seven
one-thousand-year periods from Creation, thus each “day” gets us closer to
“Shabbos” —‫ — יום שכולו שבת‬the coming of Mashiach.

Sunday (Chapter 24)

‫שיר של יום ראשון‬

:‫ ׁ ֶשבּ וֹ ָהי ּו ַה ְלוִ ִ ּים אוֹ ְמ ִרים ְּב ֵבית ַה ִּמ ְקדָּ ׁש‬,‫אשון ְּב ׁ ַש ָּבת‬

‫ — ְל ָדוִד ִמזְ מֹור‬The entire earth
belongs to Hashem. Therefore, who
will succeed here and be worthy
of blessings? Only those who are
sincere in the service of Hashem,
who use this material world for
noble goals and who deal with
others with honesty and integrity!
We hope for the day when all of
civilization will recognize Hashem
and live by these values.

On Rosh Chodesh, add Tehillim Chapter 104:

‫ברכי נפשי‬
The Majesty and Intricacy of the Creation Demonstrates the Power and
Providence of Hashem!
Speaking about the majesty of Hashem’s Creation is very nice, but how is that
relevant to Rosh Chodesh (besides the fact that the moon gets mentioned
as part of the Creation)? Bnei Yisrael follow the lunar (moon) calendar and
not the solar (sun) calendar. Why? Because the moon seems to get smaller
and smaller and almost disappears. Then, suddenly, just when you think the
moon is gone, it reappears and grows again. So, too, Bnei Yisrael — there are
many times when the nations of the world assume that we’re done, finished,
and will disappear, and then we pop up again and grow bright and strong.
The same is true with each of us. We’ve all had times when we feel we’re
not growing; we’ve become insignificant and are losing our “light.” But we

‫‪110‬‬

‫שחרית • ברכי נפשי‬

‫‪always have the ability to pop back, just like the moon does every month.‬‬
‫‪And why is that? Because the “laws of nature” and “rules of politics and‬‬
‫‪sociology” are controlled by the Master Creator! But sometimes that’s hard‬‬
‫‪to see. So every month, when that moon “miraculously” reappears, we read‬‬
‫‪this chapter of Tehillim where David HaMelech describes for us the wonders‬‬
‫‪of the created world — how Hashem takes care of the largest to the smallest,‬‬
‫‪and thus takes care of each and every one of us — allowing us to pop back up‬‬
‫‪when we’re down.‬‬

‫ָּב ְר ִכי נַ ְפ ׁ ִשי ֶאת יְ הֹוָ ה‪ ,‬יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ַֹהי ָ ּג ַד ְל ָּת ְּמאֹד‪,‬‬
‫הוֹ ד וְ ָה ָדר ָל ָב ׁ ְש ָּת‪ :‬ע ֶֹטה אוֹ ר ַּכשּׂ ַ ְל ָמה‪ ,‬נוֹ ֶטה‬
‫יעה‪ַ :‬ה ְמ ָק ֶרה ַב ַּמיִ ם ֲע ִל ּיוֹ ָתיו‪,‬‬
‫ׁ ָש ַמיִ ם ַּכיְ ִר ָ‬
‫ַהשּׂ ָ ם ָע ִבים ְרכוּבוֹ ‪ַ ,‬ה ְמ ַה ֵּל ְך ַעל ַּכנְ ֵפי רו ַּח‪:‬‬
‫ֹשה ַמ ְל ָא ָכיו רוּחוֹ ת‪ְ ,‬מ ׁ ָש ְר ָתיו ֵא ׁש ל ֵֹהט‪:‬‬
‫ע ֶׂ‬
‫יה‪ַּ ,‬בל ִּת ּמוֹ ט עוֹ ָלם וָ ֶעד‪:‬‬

‫שחרית • למנצח לבני קרח‬

112

The following is said in the house of an Avel during the week of Shivah, on days when
Tachanun is said:

‫תהילים מט‬
The focus of this chapter is on our relationship with our material possessions
and how they are to be used to help us fulfill our mission here on earth.

113 Shacharis • Michtam L’Dovid

The following is said in the house of an Avel during the week of Shivah, on days on which Tachanun
is not said:

‫תהילים טז‬
The focus of this chapter is that if we put our trust in Hashem He will protect
us, and that despite all our difficulties, all that He does is for our best.

In Israel, the following is added:

‫פטום הקטורת‬
As we transition to our work day, we focus on: (1) trusting in Hashem that only
He can make all our efforts succeed; (2) reciting the Ketores which, according
to Kabbalistic sources, brings spiritual sustenance to ourselves and the world
around us for the rest of the day; (3) the importance of using halachah to
guide all our activities; and (notwithstanding the competitive nature of school
and work) (4) the importance of peace and harmony.

‫ — ַקּוֵה ֶאל ְיהֹוָה‬Put your trust in ‫ ֵאין‬:‫ וְ ַק ֵּוה ֶאל יְ הֹוָ ה‬,‫ ֲחזַ ק וְ יַ ֲא ֵמץ ִל ֶ ּב ָך‬,‫ַק ֵּוה ֶאל יְ הֹוָ ה‬
Hashem; (and) ‫ַא ֵמץ ִל ֶּבָך‬
ֲ ‫ — ֲחזַק ְוי‬that ‫ ִּכי‬:ּ‫ וְ ֵאין צוּר ֵּכאל ֵֹהינו‬,‫ ִּכי ֵאין ִ ּב ְל ֶּת ָך‬,‫ָקדוֹ ׁש ַּכיהֹוָ ה‬
itself will give you the courage
:ּ‫ ו ִּמי צוּר זו ָּל ִתי ֱאל ֵֹהינו‬,‫ִמי ֱאלוֹ ַּה ִמ ַּב ְל ֲע ֵדי יְ הֹוָ ה‬
and strength to persevere! (And if
Hashem has not yet given you what you asked for); ‫ְקּוֵה ֶאל ְיהֹוָה‬
ַ ‫ — ו‬continue to place your
trust in Hashem (for there is no other power to rely on but Hashem alone)!
‫ֵאֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫ — ֵאין ּכ‬There is no other
power like Hashem our King!
Therefore, we thank You, for
You are the Source of all blessing
and salvation. And it was to You
alone that (in the times of the Beis
Hamikdash) we offered the Ketores.

In Israel, on days when the Torah is not read, some have the custom of saying Barchu.

:‫ָּב ְרכ ּו ֶאת יְ הֹוָ ה ַה ְּמב ָֹר ְך‬

:‫ ָּברו ְּך יְ הֹוָ ה ַה ְּמב ָֹר ְך ְלעוֹ ָלם וָ ֶעד‬:‫קהל וחזן‬

‫שחרית • שש זכירות‬

116

‫שש זכירות‬
As You Go through Your Day, Don’t Forget!
Though there are siddurim that list just four, and some list ten, this list of six
“remembrances” is the most common custom. Note that there are three positive
elements to focus on: Hashem redeemed us from Egypt (and will in the future);
Hashem spoke directly to us at Har Sinai (which obligates us); and Shabbos,
which reminds us of Creation (and thus His providence). The three negative
elements tell us to always be on guard about: the Golden Calf (sins against
Hashem); Miriam’s lashon hara (sins against others); and Amalek (nations who
do not fear Hashem, no matter how seemingly “cultured,” can act with great
cruelty). The point is not to mumble them like some magic formula! Rather, the
idea is to focus on these key points and use them as your guide during your day.

ָ ‫את ָך ֵמ ֶא ֶרץ ִמ ְצ ַריִ ם ּכֹל יְ ֵמי ַח ֶ ּי‬
:‫יך‬
ְ ‫ְל ַמ ַען ִּתזְ ּכֹר ֶאת יוֹ ם ֵצ‬
‫ — יציאת מצרים‬We must remember that Hashem took us out of Egypt with great
miracles, which gives us hope that He will again redeem us and take us out of this
final galus!

‫ּשמֹר נַ ְפ ׁ ְש ָך ְמאֹד ּ ֶפן ִּת ׁ ְש ַּכח ֶאת ַהדְּ ָב ִרים ֲא ׁ ֶשר‬
ְ ׁ ‫ַרק ִה ּׁ ָש ֶמר ְל ָך ו‬
ָ ֶ‫יך וְ הוֹ ַד ְע ָּתם ְל ָבנ‬
ָ ‫יך ו ֶּפן יָ סוּר ּו ִמ ְּל ָב ְב ָך ּכֹל יְ ֵמי ַח ֶ ּי‬
ָ ֶ‫ָרא ּו ֵעינ‬
‫יך וְ ִל ְבנֵ י‬
ָ ‫ יוֹ ם ֲא ׁ ֶשר ָע ַמ ְד ָּת ִל ְפנֵ י יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֶֹה‬:‫יך‬
ָ ֶ‫ָבנ‬
:‫יך ְּבח ֵֹרב‬
‫ — מעמד הר סיני‬We must remember the unique defining moment of Bnei Yisrael,
when Hashem spoke to three million of us at Har Sinai, and gave us His Torah.

‫ ֲא ׁ ֶשר‬:‫את ֶכם ִמ ִּמ ְצ ָריִ ם‬
ְ ‫שה ְל ָך ֲע ָמ ֵלק ַּבדֶּ ֶר ְך ְּב ֵצ‬
ׂ ָ ‫זָ כוֹ ר ֵאת ֲא ׁ ֶשר ָע‬
ָ
ָ
‫ָק ְר ָך ַּבדֶּ ֶר ְך וַ יְ זַ ֵ ּנב ְּבך ָּכל ַה ֶ ּנ ֱח ׁ ָש ִלים ַא ַח ֶריך וְ ַא ָּתה ָעיֵ ף וְ יָ גֵ ַע וְ לֹא יָ ֵרא‬
ָ ‫יך ְל ָך ִמ ָּכל אֹיְ ֶב‬
ָ ‫יח יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֶֹה‬
‫יך ִמ ּ ָס ִביב ָּב ָא ֶרץ‬
ַ ִ‫ וְ ָהיָ ה ְּב ָהנ‬:‫ֱאל ִֹהים‬
ָ
ָ ‫ֲא ׁ ֶשר יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֶֹה‬
‫יך נ ֵֹתן ְלך נַ ֲח ָלה ְל ִר ׁ ְש ָּת ּה ִּת ְמ ֶחה ֶאת זֵ ֶכר ֲע ָמ ֵלק‬
:‫ִמ ַּת ַחת ַה ּׁ ָש ָמיִ ם לֹא ִּת ׁ ְש ָּכח‬
‫ — מעשה עמלק‬We must remember the evil of Amalek attacking us for no reason
right after the miracle of the splitting of the sea. We are obligated to wipe out evil!

ָ ‫זְ כֹר ַאל ִּת ׁ ְש ַּכח ֵאת ֲא ׁ ֶשר ִה ְק ַצ ְפ ָּת ֶאת יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֶֹה‬
:‫יך ַּב ִּמ ְד ָּבר‬
‫ — מעשה העגל‬We must remember how we angered Hashem when we attempted to
serve Him our own way, rather than as prescribed by His Torah, notwithstanding
all our good intentions!

‫את ֶכם‬
ְ ‫יך ְל ִמ ְריָ ם ַּבדֶּ ֶר ְך ְּב ֵצ‬

‫ — מרים‬We must remember what Hashem did to Miriam: Her punishment for
speaking lashon hara about Moshe, even though it was a seemingly minor

117 Shacharis • Shloshah Asar Ikarim

comment, and the great reward she was given (that the entire nation waited for her
for seven days) for her seemingly minor chessed that she waited to see what would
happen to baby Moshe while he was hidden in a basket in the water.

: ֹ‫זָ כוֹ ר ֶאת יוֹ ם ַה ּׁ ַש ָּבת ְל ַקדְּ ׁשו‬
‫ — שבת‬We must remember that it is the power and sanctity of Shabbos that gives
us the “energy” to remain spiritually strong during the week.

‫שלשה עשר עיקרים‬
No Matter What Happens, No Matter Where We Are,
This Is What We Believe!
What’s popular is not always right, and what’s right is not always popular!
Not only must we make sure to “do” what is correct, but we need to also
stay firm in our core beliefs and value system, which, in many cases, are not
popular and are the opposite of what the “world out there” believes. As with
the Six Remembrances, the point is not to mumble the Thirteen Principles
like some magic formula! Rather, the idea is to think about what they mean
to you and to use them as your guide during your day. The commentaries
have pointed out that they break down into three basic categories: belief in
Hashem (1–5), the truth of Torah (6–9), and our responsibilities as Bnei Yisrael
and our ultimate reward (10–13).

I believe with perfect faith that
Hashem created everything and
guides all creations.

Instant Insight: The context of “‫ִיתי ְיהֹוָה‬
ִ ‫יׁשּוע ְתָך ִקו‬
ָ ‫ — ִל‬For Your salvation I hope, Hashem!”

is the conclusion of Yaakov Avinu’s brachah to Dan. Yaakov blessed the tribe of Dan,
and specifically their famous descendant Shimshon, with great military strength
and cunning, and that the shevet as a whole and Shimshon in particular should
be victorious in the many battles against the Philistines. Nevertheless, Yaakov
concluded his brachah by proclaiming that it is only “for Your salvation I hope,
Hashem.” No matter how strong we are and no matter how great our material
blessings (and no matter how many of this morning’s requests Hashem grants
us!), we must put our trust only in Hashem, and never forget that our success is in
His Hands!

119 Birkas Ha’mazon

You Ate a Good Meal? It’s a Miracle Like Manna from Heaven!
Say Thank You!

‫ברכת המזון‬
Bentchaphobia: “I don’t want bread — I don’t want to have to bentch!” Why do
we go to great lengths to avoid having to bentch? The simple answer is that
bentching is long and we’re in a rush. But I’d like to take it a bit deeper and
offer two thoughts on this subject for you to consider. And they apply not
only to the full Birkas Ha’mazon, but to Al Ha’michyah and Borei Nefashos
as well.
1. In our world of plenty (“When I was your age … ”), it’s so easy to take stuff
for granted. We go to the store and find tons of all kinds of food right there
for us — in all seasons. What does it take for all that food to get to the store?
A lot of miracles! The sun has to rise, it must rain at the right time and the
right amount, seeds must grow — you get the idea. And there are so many
miracles involved in us having the ability to eat the food, taste it, digest it,
etc. These miracles are no less amazing than the manna in the desert. It’s
just that we’re used to these miracles since they happen all the time (except
when there are hurricanes, floods, sickness — again, you get the idea). That’s
why we don’t feel the need to spend all that time bentching — it’s a burden
for “no real good reason.” This is similar to Pesukei D’Zimrah, which many
people find annoying — “Why do I have to praise Hashem so much? Does
He (chas v’shalom) struggle with His self-esteem?” Here too, we so quickly
take our world and our miraculous history for granted. Hashem does not
need our bentching, and He does not need our Pesukei D’Zimrah. We need
to say it in order to better appreciate — and thus enjoy — all that we have!
2. We don’t like to owe other people favors. We don’t like to be beholden to
others. “I’m fine, I can do it all myself!” But we can’t do anything without
Hashem’s help! And that idea can make some people feel uncomortable
because they like to be in control. By bentching (and again — the same
for Pesukei D’Zimrah) we are helping ourselves focus on the concept
that though we must do as much as we can, in the end, nothing happens
without Hashem’s help.
On weekdays, some start with the following:

‫תהילים קלז‬
The focus of this chapter is that even after the destruction of the Beis
Hamikdash, when Yerushalayim was physically destroyed by Bavel, its
spirit still lives on in all our hearts! As we enjoy our meal and our material
abundance, we remember that we are not fully “home”; we must not forget
that Yerushalayim is not yet what it should be!

On Shabbos and Yom Tov start with the following:

‫תהילים קכו‬
The focus of this chapter is on the Era of Mashiach and our return to
Yerushalayim, when the power and providence of Hashem in our individual
and national lives will finally begin to become clear to us and the world.

‫ — ִׁשיר ַה ַּמ ֲעלֹות‬When Hashem takes

confusion will begin to clear). We will
sing praises to Hashem, and the
nations of the world will proclaim:
“Hashem has done great miracles
for His chosen nation” (that’s
us!). Please, Hashem, take us out
of our galus! ‫ַהּז ְֹר ִעים ְּב ִד ְמ ָעה ְּב ִרּנָה‬

There Is Power in Numbers!

‫זימון‬
:‫ ַרבּ וֹ ַתי נְ ָב ֵר ְך‬:‫המזמן אומר‬
(The Leader calls us to action and says to us:) We must make the world realize (by
how we speak and how we conduct ourselves daily) that Hashem is the Source of
everything (and certainly of the meal we just ate!).

:‫ יְ ִהי ׁ ֵשם יְ הֹוָ ה ְמב ָֹר ְך ֵמ ַע ָּתה וְ ַעד עוֹ ָלם‬:‫המסובים עונים‬
(And we respond:) May everyone (including me!), everywhere, and forever, see that
Hashem is the Source of everything!

(The Leader responds:) May everyone (including me!), everywhere, and forever, see
that Hashem is the Source of everything!

The Second Brachah:

Eretz Yisrael!

‫ברכת הארץ‬
‫ — נֹודֶה ְּלָך‬We thank You,
Hashem, for: giving us a
wonderful and spacious
land; taking us out of
Egypt and freeing us
from slavery; giving us
the mitzvah of bris milah;
for Your Torah that You
taught us; for Your mitzvos
that You gave us; for life,
goodness, and kindness
that You granted us; and
for continually taking care
of us, constantly!

FAQ:
What? It certainly
doesn’t look like
Hashem “provides
all of us with all we
need”! There are
people out there
really suffering!
ANSWER:
That is the classic
question of tzaddik
v’ra lo — why the
righteous suffer!

)‫(— ַר ֶחם (נָא‬Please) Hashem, have
mercy on Your nation Bnei Yisrael
(and take us out of galus), and on Your
city of Yerushalayim and on Zion
(that it should be rebuilt) and on the
royal house of David HaMelech (that
Mashiach should come) and on the Beis
Hamikdash (that it should be rebuilt).
Hashem, our Father Who takes care
of us, please feed and sustain us, and
send us relief from all our difficulties.
And we should never need the help
of others, but rather only Your direct
help, which is always available and
generous. This way we will never feel
ashamed or humiliated.

FAQ:
It certainly doesn’t
look like Hashem
“does good
to everyone”!
There are people
out there really
suffering! And
my life is a mess!
I certainly don’t
see “prosperity,
success, relief from
our troubles … ”!
ANSWER:
Correct! Once
again, that is
the classic (and
very important)
question of tzaddik
v’ra lo, why the
righteous suffer!

in the eyes of people.

On Rosh Chodesh: ‫ — ָה ַר ֲח ָמן‬May

renew this coming month for us for
good and for blessing.

FAQ:
“Never did I see
a tzaddik who
was forsaken (by
Hashem), whose
children had to
go begging for
food” — but that’s
not what it looks
like!
ANSWER:
Correct again!
And once
again, that is
the classic (and
very important)
question of tzaddik
v’ra lo, why the
righteous suffer!
See Appendix 2.

ַ ‫ — ז‬I was young, and have now grown old, and never did I see a tzaddik who was
forsaken (by Hashem), whose children had to go begging for food. Hashem will give
strength to His nation; Hashem will bless his nation with peace.
You Ate a Piece of Cake, Had Some Wine, and/or Fruit from the Seven
Species? It’s Also a Miracle like Manna from Heaven! Say Thank You!

‫ברכה מעין שלש — על המחיה‬
Yes, even cake is a miracle! See Introduction to Birkas Ha’mazon above on 119.

‫ — ָּברּוְך ַא ָּתה ְיהֹוָה‬You, Hashem, are the
Source of everything; ‫ֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫ — ֱא‬You
take care of us; ‫עֹולם‬
ָ ‫ — ֶמ ֶלְך ָה‬and You
rule the entire world. Thank you for:
the nourishment and sustenance/
the vine and fruit of the vine/the
tree and the fruit of the tree; ‫ְעל‬
ַ‫ו‬
‫נּובת ַה ָּׂשדֶה‬
ַ ‫ — ְּת‬and for the produce
of the field, and for the precious,
good, and spacious land that You
have graciously given as a heritage
to our ancestors, so that they may
eat of its fruit and be satiated with
its goodness. Hashem, have mercy
on Your nation Bnei Yisrael, on
Your city Yerushalayim, on Zion
where Your Divine Presence rests,
on Your Mizbei’ach and on the Beis
Hamikdash. Rebuild Yerushalayim
speedily in our lifetime, and may
You bring us up there and we will
rejoice in its rebuilding! And may we
eat from its fruit, be satisfied by all
its goodness, and we will thank You
for all that with sanctity and purity.
On Shabbos: Please refresh and
fortify us on this day of Shabbos.
On Rosh Chodesh: Remember us for
good on this day of Rosh Chodesh.
On Yom Tov: Grant us simchah on
this day of Pesach/Sukkos/Shavuos.

‫ — ִּכי ַא ָּתה‬For You, Hashem, are

the Source of everything; (and we

the nourishment/fruit of the vine/

‫ָּברו ְּך ַא ָּתה יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֵֹהינ ּו ֶמ ֶל ְך‬

:‫ְל ַה ֲחיוֹ ת ָּב ֶהם נֶ ֶפ ׁש ָּכל ָחי‬
:‫ָּברו ְּך ֵחי ָהעוֹ ָל ִמים‬

‫— ָּברּוְך ַא ָּתה ְיהֹוָה‬You, Hashem, are the
Source of everything; ‫ֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫— ֱא‬You
take care of us; ‫עֹולם‬
ָ ‫— ֶמלְֶך ָה‬and You
rule the entire world; ‫נְפׁשֹות‬
ָ ‫ּבֹורֵא‬
‫ְח ְסרֹונָן‬
ֶ ‫— ַרּבֹות ו‬You are the Creator of
numerous living things and all they
need to survive; ‫את‬
ָ ‫ַעל ָּכל ַמה ֶׁש ָּב ָר‬
‫(— ְל ַה ֲחיֹות ָּב ֶהם נֶפֶׁש ָּכל ָחי‬we thank you)
for all the things You have created
with which to sustain the life of every living being. ‫עֹול ִמים‬
ָ ‫— ָּברּוְך ֵחי ָה‬You are the Source
of life of the worlds.

We translated ‫ ֶח ְסרֹונָן‬as thanking Hashem Who created all that we need
to survive, all that we are lacking. But literally, the phrase really means that we are
thanking Hashem Who created numerous living things and their deficiencies! Why
would we thank Hashem for creating us with deficiencies and therefore needing
stuff? As we explained at length in Appendix 1, the most important thing we need
is to create a relationship with Hashem and talk to Him on a regular basis. Having
deficiencies (we are hungry and thirsty) pushes us to talk to Him! Let’s be honest. If
we were created completely self-sufficient, we would quickly forget about Hashem!
In addition, thanks to the fact that we all have ‫ח ְסרֹונָן‬,
ֶ we have the opportunity to do
chessed with each other! This helps us all bond and create a more loving community.

‫מנחה‬
The Hardest Time to Daven Is When You Need to Daven the Most!
Why is the afternoon tefillah called Minchah? Shacharis comes from
‫ — שחר‬morning, and Maariv comes from ‫ — ערב‬evening, but “Minchah” means a
gift! (True, there was a ‫מנחה‬/meal offering in the afternoon, but there also was
one in the morning!) In addition, Chazal attach great importance to Minchah.
Moshe successfully davened to Hashem to forgive the sin of the golden calf at
Minchah time (FYI: ‫ עגל‬equals 103, and so does ‫ ;)מנחה‬and Eliyahu, in his battle
against the idol worshippers, was also answered late afternoon at Minchah
time. So what is the significance of Minchah and Minchah-time? (NOTE: Though
technically one can daven Minchah once it is a halachic half-hour after noon,
the “tefillah of Minchah” was set up parallel to the Tamid Shel Bein HaArbayim,
the korban that was brought right before sunset as the day ended.)
Twilight represents a time of lack of clarity and vision. Most traffic accidents
happen then! When it’s dark, you know it’s dark, so either you turn on your
headlights or you’re more careful. In the morning, things are clear. But dusk/
late afternoon is that in-between time. You think you can see, but it’s not
really that clear. We all have those times in our lives, and that is when we are
most prone to stumbling and doing things we will be disappointed about in
the “morning” when we can look back with clarity! Thus “Minchah-time” is
the time when we most need to reconnect to Hashem. And practically, it is
usually at a very inconvenient part of the day when we are really busy with a
million and one things. So emotionally, it’s a hard time to connect, since we

133 Minchah • Ashrei

lack clarity; and practically, it’s a hard time to connect, since we are so busy
thinking about ourselves.
When we stop, step back, get ahold of ourselves, and proclaim our true
feelings — that ‫אשרי יושבי ביתך‬, those who are connected to the Presence of
Hashem are the happiest people — we are giving a great gift, a ‫מנחה‬, to Hashem,
by acknowledging Him when it is hardest to do so. And really, we are giving
ourselves a great gift — the peace of mind that we are not alone, no matter
what is going in our lives at this moment. The hardest time to daven is when
you need to daven the most!
I Am Happy I Can Interrupt My Day and Daven to Hashem

‫אשרי‬
‫ אשרי‬follows the aleph-beis. The significance is that in all areas of our life
(aleph to tav), personally and nationally, we need Hashem and His help, we
are supported by Him, and His providence is constant. And this is true not
only when our lives seem clear and bright (Shacharis — the morning), but also
when things seem to be getting dark and not so bright or clear (Minchah — as
the sun sets). Because of its importance, we will explain it line by line.
For a different set of Instant Insights on Ashrei, please see the Ashrei of Shacharis.

‫ עוֹ ד‬,‫ית ָך‬
ֶ ‫ַא ׁ ְש ֵרי יוֹ ׁ ְש ֵבי ֵב‬
‫ ַא ׁ ְש ֵרי ָה ָעם‬:‫יְ ַה ְללו ָּך ּ ֶס ָלה‬
‫ ַא ׁ ְש ֵרי ָה ָעם ׁ ֶשיְ הֹוָ ה‬, ֹ‫ׁ ֶש ָּכ ָכה לו‬
:‫ֱאל ָֹהיו‬

‫ ֲארוֹ ִמ ְמ ָך ֱאלוֹ ַהי‬,‫ְּת ִה ָּלה ְל ָדוִ ד‬

Instant Insight:
It does not say
that Hashem picks
up the “‫— ”ּנ ְֹפ ִלים‬
those who have

Why doesn’t

Because falling is

“The tzaddik falls

getting back up is
needed to build our
“spiritual muscles,”
and therefore we
must do that on
our own. But He
does support and
help us!

upward.

OUR REQUESTS

‫שמנה עשרה‬
If anything I do today is going to be successful, I need to
ask Hashem for help!
Opening Statement

:ּ‫ִּכי ׁ ֵשם יְ הֹוָ ה ֶא ְק ָרא ָהב ּו ג ֶֹדל ֵלאל ֵֹהינו‬
‫אֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫ — ִּכי ֵׁשם ְיהֹוָה ֶא ְק ָרא ָהבּו גֹדֶל ֵל‬When I proclaim the providence
of Hashem, (which I am now going to focus on in Minchah) it will
become clear to all that everyone must ascribe greatness to (and
therefore praise) Hashem, our God.
:‫ש ָפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח ו ִּפי יַ ִ ּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶת ָך‬
ׂ ְ ,‫ֲאדֹנָ י‬

FAQ:
Why am I asking
Hashem for stuff?
Hashem knows
what I want, and He
also knows what
I really need! If I
really need it and
am supposed to
have it, He would
have given it to me!
FAQ:

‫ּופי י ִַּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶתָך‬
ִ ‫ׂפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח‬
ָ ‫ ְש‬,‫( — ֲאדֹנָי‬I am standing here because I do
want to daven, create a relationship, and thank You for all the good
You do for me — which is sometimes hard to see or understand — and I
want to ask You for the help I need. But that does not come easy to me
for so many reasons. Therefore, I am asking, please) Hashem, open
my lips and help me express myself properly.

Why should I
bother making
requests from
Hashem? I’ve asked
Him many times
before for stuff,
and He keeps on
saying no!

Both very crucial
questions for a
serious Ben Torah
and Bas Yisrael! See
Appendix 1.

Instant Insight:
The terms ‫עוזר‬, ‫מושיע‬,
and ‫ מגן‬represent
three levels of help
that we need in
our lives — from the
least to the most.
‫“ — עוזר‬help,” is
when we are on the
right track, doing
what we need to
do in the best way
possible, and we
ask Hashem to
please help us that
all should work out,
since nothing will
come to fruition
without His help.
‫“ — מושיע‬salvation,”
is when we are
completely at a
loss; we throw up
our hands and
say to Hashem,
“Save me, I have
no idea what to
do next!” The third
and highest level,
‫“ — מגן‬protect,” is
when we think
we’re on the right
track and think we
know what we’re
doing, but in truth
we are completely
off base! We ask
Hashem, “If I’m
going in the wrong
direction — ‫— מגן‬
protect me from
myself!”

‫קדושה‬
Let us praise Hashem like the angels do!

Unlike angels who have a higher understanding of Hashem, we humans cannot
understand “Who” Hashem is — His very essence (represented by the word
“‫)”אותו‬. All we can perceive is what He does — referred to as “‫שמך‬.” Nevertheless,
in Kedushah we will praise Hashem like the angels do. Since we can only do
this when we are at the level of a tzibbur — a unified community — we therefore
only say this with a minyan.

we are deserving); for You, Hashem,
listen to tefillos and pleadings.

ִ — Do not
turn us away empty-handed from
You, our King!
(Now stop for a second. Here is your big chance! This is where you may add any and all
personal requests you have! Whatever is in your heart and on your mind, don’t be shy to
speak it out now, Hashem understands all languages and He listens!)

Instant Insight:
Why do we say
“listen to our voice
(‫ ?”)קולנו‬Shouldn’t
we ask Hashem to
listen to the words
we say? What we
are asking Hashem
is: “I may not be so
articulate and may
not know how to
say what I really
feel. But I hope You
can tell from the
sound of my voice
that I am doing
the best I can to
communicate with
You!”
Crucial
Instant Insight:
We asked Hashem
to “accept our
tefillos,” so what
are we adding by
requesting, “Do
not turn us away
empty-handed”?
Of course, we hope
that Hashem will
grant us what we
have requested,
but sometimes,
for reasons known
only to Him,
Hashem must say
no. We therefore
ask Hashem that if
the answer to our
tefillah must be a
no, then at least do
not send us away
feeling “emptyhanded” — with
a feeling of total
rejection. Let our
hearts be filled with
emunah that we
had the amazing
zechus and
opportunity to talk
one-on-one with
Hashem and He
heard our tefillah,
even if the best
thing for us right
now is a no.

Instant Insight:

In a minyan: When the chazzan says Modim, we say the following:

‫מודים דרבנן‬
When thanking Hashem, an “Amen” is not sufficient!

‫ַחנּו ָלְך‬
ְ ‫מֹודים ֲאנ‬
ִ — We thank ‫ ָׁש ַא ָּתה הוּא‬,‫מוֹ ִדים ֲאנַ ְחנ ּו ָל ְך‬
You, Hashem, for You ,ּ‫יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֵֹהינ ּו וֵ אל ֵֹהי ֲאבוֹ ֵתינו‬
are our God. You control
everything
and
have ‫ יוֹ ְצ ֵרנ ּו יוֹ ֵצר‬,‫ֱאל ֵֹהי ָכל ָ ּב ָׁשר‬
ִׁ ‫ְּב ֵר‬
taken care of Bnei Yisrael ‫ ְ ּב ָרכוֹ ת וְ הוֹ ָדאוֹ ת‬:‫אשית‬
throughout our history ‫ ַעל‬,‫ְל ִׁש ְמ ָך ַה ָ ּגדוֹ ל וְ ַה ָּקדוֹ ׁש‬
starting from the time
‫ ֵּכן ְּת ַחיֵ ּ נ ּו‬,ּ‫יתנ ּו וְ ִקיַ ּ ְמ ָּתנו‬
ָ ִ‫ֶׁש ֶה ֱחי‬
of the Avos. You are the
ּ
Creator of all humanity, ‫ וְ ֶת ֱאסוֹ ף ָ ּג ֻליוֹ ֵתינ ּו‬,ּ‫ו ְּת ַקיְ ֵמנו‬
ָ ‫ ִל ְׁשמֹר ֻח ֶּק‬,‫ְל ַח ְצרוֹ ת ָק ְד ֶׁש ָך‬
of the entire universe, and ‫יך‬
You specifically formed us
‫וְ ַל ֲעשׂ וֹ ת ְרצוֹ נֶ ָך ו ְּל ָע ְב ְד ָך ְב ֵל ָבב‬
as Your nation — for which
ְ
we thank You, and for our ,‫ ַעל ֶׁש ֲאנַ ְחנ ּו מוֹ ִדים ָלך‬,‫ָׁש ֵלם‬

:‫ָּברו ְּך ֵאל ַההוֹ ָדאוֹ ת‬

ֵ ‫ּות ַקי‬
ְ ‫ — ֵּכן ְּת ַחיֵּנּו‬we thank You
now for the fact that we will continue to survive, and that You
will bring all the exiles to the (rebuilt) Beis Hamikdash where
we will all serve You with a complete heart (with no doubts or
questions). ‫מֹודים ָלְך‬
ִ ‫ַחנּו‬
ְ ‫ — ַעל ֶׁש ֲאנ‬We thank You for instilling in
us the concept and (crucial) character trait of gratitude! ‫ָּברּוְך ֵאל‬
‫הֹודאֹות‬
ָ ‫ — ַה‬You, Hashem, are the Source of everything, to Whom
thanksgiving is due.

By all other brachos
of the Shemoneh
Esrei, we simply
respond “Amen”
to the chazzan’s
brachah. Why here
must we say our
own “Modim,” and
an Amen is not
sufficient? All other
brachos are requests.
When you are with
someone else and
they put in a request
for something
important, saying
simply, “And me
too, I also agree!” is
fine. He represents
you. That is “Amen.”
But if someone is
really bursting with
gratitude, especially
to Hashem, for “our
lives, which are
entrusted into Your
Hand” and “for our
miraculous survival
till today,” then Amen
is impossible. No
one can “represent”
your inner feeling
of gratitude! It must
burst forth! Thus,
Modim D’Rabbanan!

‫ָׂשם ְלָך ָׁשלֹום‬
ֵ ‫ִּׂשא ְיהֹוָה ָּפנָיו ֵא ֶליָך ְוי‬
ָ ‫ — י‬May you feel the constant Presence
of Hashem, and thus be at peace (within yourself).

Generally, we think
of “‫ — שלום‬peace”
as meaning people
getting along with
each other. But if
so, then the third
brachah should
have said “‫וישם לכם‬
‫ — שלום‬Hashem will
give peace to you,”
plural, i.e., to the
group. Why is the
word “‫ — לך‬to you,”
singular, used? The
singular is used
because you cannot
get along with
others if you first
do not get along
with yourself! Thus,
this brachah is to
the individual — that
each person should
have inner serenity
and peace. Only
then can we live in
peace with others.

153 Minchah • Shemoneh Esrei

‫שלום‬
Inner, outer, and communal peace

‫ — ָׁשלֹום ָרב‬Hashem, grant abundant
(inner and communal) peace upon
Your nation Bnei Yisrael forever, for
You are the Master of peace. And
may it be favorable in Your eyes
to bless Your people, Bnei Yisrael,
at all times and at every moment,
with Your peace (protection from
wars and dangers).

(Peace between one another:) ‫ ֻּכ ָּלנּו ְּכ ֶא ָחד‬,‫ — ָּב ְר ֵכנּו ָא ִבינּו‬Our Father , bless all of us
together to become one unified nation, by allowing us to all equally see Your
Presence (and thanks to such clarity, discord will cease!). For You, Hashem, when we
were in Your direct Divine Presence (at Har Sinai), gave us a living Torah (which
teaches us how to live together in harmony), and You also gave us a love of doing
kindness and righteousness. (This all leads to everyone sharing) their blessings
with each other, living with compassion, and thus having a full life and living in
peace (by forgiving others).
(Peace from war and danger:) ‫ — וְטֹוב ְּב ֵעינֶיָך ְל ָב ֵרְך ֶאת ַע ְּמָך‬And may it be favorable in
Your eyes to bless Your people, Bnei Yisrael, at all times and at every moment,
with Your peace (protection from wars and dangers).

A SPECIAL TIME, SPECIAL REQUESTS

‫אבינו מלכנו‬
On fast days (except Tishah B’Av) and during the Aseres Yemei Teshuvah, we add
Avinu Malkeinu before Tachanun:

Is Hashem our kind and compassionate Father (‫ )אבינו‬Who understands our
failings, or is He our King (‫ )מלכנו‬with demands and rules? The answer is — He is
both! Hashem is our King, with a special mission for each of us, with rules and
structures to help us maximize our spiritual potential to fulfill that mission.
And we must never lose sight of our responsibilities and the end goal — those
highest rungs of the ladder we climb in our avodas Hashem. Our decisions do
have very serious consequences!
Yet, importantly, He is also our Father Who understands that at times we
stumble, lose our footing, and get sidetracked from our goal. Through all
this, He still supports us and gives us multiple opportunities to get back on
track. On these special days, when we think about who we really are, what our
mission is, what our real potential is, and take stock of our progress, we need
to focus on both those aspects of our relationship with Him. By doing that, we
will be worthy of Hashem giving us what we are asking for so that we can get
back up and be the people He created us to be.
NOTE: The Hebrew of this tefillah is quite simple and straightforward. I urge you to say each line slowly
in the original.

ָ ֶ‫ָא ִבינ ּו ַמ ְל ֵּכנ ּו ָח ָטאנ ּו ְל ָפנ‬
:‫יך‬
Our Father, our King, we have sinned against You.

:‫ָא ִבינ ּו ַמ ְל ֵּכנ ּו ֵאין ָלנ ּו ֶמ ֶל ְך ֶא ָּלא ָא ָּתה‬

Our Father, our King, (even though we have messed up, still we accept and publicly
proclaim that) we have no King besides You!

Instant Insight:
Regarding the
“book of merits”
or remembering us
“for merits”: Either
one has merits or
they do not! If they
do, they are in the
book; and if not,
what is Hashem
supposed to do?
What does this
mean? The idea
here is that Hashem
could arrange your
life so that it’s easy
for you to pick up
merits and good
deeds (someone in
front of you drops
something and
you pick it up!) or
the opposite — you
simply do not have
easy opportunities
to do good deeds.
We are asking
Hashem to please
remember us and
write us in the
book of those who
will have many
opportunities to do
mitzvos!

Maybe Today So Far Hasn’t Been My Best, but I Realize
That — and I’m Asking for Divine Favor!

‫תחנון‬
For a list of when Tachanun is omitted, see Appendix 12:9.
The general custom is that women do not say Tachanun.
Why? The most common explanation is that Tachanun,
similar to Maariv, was originally an optional tefillah that
women did not accept upon themselves.

‫מעריב‬
My Day Is Ending, but the New One Just Started — Time to Daven!
In the morning, we awaken and begin our day by affirming our commitment to
our mission (the Shema) and we make our requests (Shemoneh Esrei) for what
we need spiritually and materially in order to fulfill our mission successfully.
Now, we end our day and set the tone for the next day (since each day really
begins the night before) by once again affirming our commitment to our
mission (the Shema) and again putting in our requests for the spiritual and
material things we need to be successful. Still, in our heart of hearts we know
that we did not really use all that Hashem gave us (our talents, character
traits, our neshamah, and spiritual gifts) in the best way possible. Therefore,
we start by focusing on the fact that Hashem is ‫ — והוא רחום יכפר עון‬He is allmerciful and ready to give us another day and another chance! With that
confidence, we can stand in front of Him and declare our readiness to try
again to maximize our potential. That gives us the ability to once again ask
Hashem for the things we need, even though we wasted some of them today!
Hashem Is Always Ready to Give Us Another Day and Another Chance!

‫והוא רחום‬
‫—וְהּוא ַרחּום י ְַכּפֵר ָעֹון‬And (because)
Hashem is merciful, He repeatedly
forgives our sins and does not destroy
us! Hashem, please save us and
answer us when we call out to You!

,‫וְ הוּא ַרחוּם יְ ַכ ּ ֵפר ָעוֹן וְ לֹא יַ ׁ ְש ִחית‬
‫ ולֹא יָ ִעיר ָּכל‬, ֹ‫וְ ִה ְר ָּבה ְל ָה ׁ ִשיב ַא ּפו‬
‫ ַה ֶּמ ֶל ְך יַ ֲענֵ נ ּו‬,‫יעה‬
ָ ‫ יְ הֹוָ ה הוֹ ׁ ִש‬: ֹ‫ֲח ָמתו‬
:ּ‫ְביוֹ ם ָק ְר ֵאנו‬

Our Mission in Simple Terms

‫ברכו‬
The entire world should recognize that Hashem is the Source of all and “bless His
Name” (i.e., realize that all Hashem does, and all that He commands, is perfect
and for the ultimate best).

:‫ ָּב ְרכ ּו ֶאת יְ הֹוָ ה ַה ְּמב ָֹר ְך‬:‫חזן‬

‫( — ָּב ְרכּו ֶאת ְיהֹוָה ַה ְּמב ָֹרְך‬The chazzan calls us to action and says to us:) We must make the
world realize (by how we speak and how we conduct ourselves daily) that Hashem is the
Source of all, and that everything He does is perfect and for the ultimate best.

:‫ ָּברו ְּך יְ הֹוָ ה ַה ְּמב ָֹר ְך ְלעוֹ ָלם וָ ֶעד‬:‫קהל וחזן‬

‫ָעד‬
ֶ ‫עֹולם ו‬
ָ ‫( — ָּברּוְך ְיהֹוָה ַה ְּמב ָֹרְך ְל‬and we respond, followed by the chazzan himself:) Yes, we
pledge to make the world realize (by how we speak and how we conduct ourselves daily)
that Hashem is the Source of all, and that everything He does is perfect and for the
ultimate best, for all eternity!

Our Mission Statement

‫קריאת שמע וברכותיה‬
Chazal structured the evening Shema by having us say
two brachos before we say the Shema, and two brachos
afterward. Then we will be ready for our one-on-one with
Hashem — the Shemoneh Esrei.

‫קריאת שמע — עול מלכות שמים‬

‫ ויאמר‬/ ‫ והיה‬/ ‫ ואהבת‬/ ‫ ברוך שם‬/ ‫שמע‬
There are many levels of meanings to this crucial pasuk,
which we should have in mind as we proclaim the essence
of all we believe. Here we will present two of the most basic
concepts one should have in mind when reciting the Shema.
If you are davening without a minyan, add the following three words:

‫ֶא ָמן‬
ֱ ‫ֵאל ֶמ ֶלְך נ‬
Hashem is a merciful, fair, and trustworthy King.

‫ש ָר ֵא֑ל יְ הֹוָ ֥ה ֱאל ֵֹה֖ינ ּו יְ הֹוָ ֥ה ׀ ֶא ָ ֽחד׃‬

‫ִׂש ָר ֵאל‬
ְ ‫ — ְׁש ַמע י‬As a proud member of Bnei Yisrael, I publicly
proclaim; that ‫ — ְיהֹוָה‬the Eternal God, Who always was, is,
and will be; ‫ֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫ — ֱא‬I accept as our personal, all-powerful God
(with all the responsibilities that come with that!); ‫ — ְיהֹוָה‬and that
God, Who controls all aspects of my life and of the universe; is
‫ — ֶא ָחד‬the One and Only controlling power.
In addition, one should have in mind:

‫ִׂש ָר ֵאל‬
ְ ‫— ְׁש ַמע י‬As a proud member of Bnei Yisrael, I publicly
proclaim; that ‫— ְיהֹוָה‬the Name that represents God’s attribute
of mercy; ‫ֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫— ֱא‬is the same God as Elokim, the Name that
represents His attribute of strict judgment. ‫— ְיהֹוָה ֶא ָחד‬It is all the
same God. And all is merciful, since all that He does is for our
good (just that sometimes the “medicine” is bitter).
Quietly:

and rulership be increased (so all humans will recognize His
existence and power); ‫ָעד‬
ֶ ‫עֹולם ו‬
ָ ‫ — ְל‬forever and ever.
Next, the Torah describes the obligation of each individual:

Important
Instant Insight:
The idea of a “yoke”
in the concept of “‫עול‬
‫ — מלכות שמים‬accepting
the yoke of kingdom of
Heaven” seems to carry
an image of mitzvos
being a heavy and
difficult burden that we
shlep around on our
backs our whole life. But
that is the superficial
understanding. True,
at times mitzvos seem
“heavy” and difficult,
and we are obligated
to persevere, but the
choice of the term
“‫ — עול‬yoke” carries a
profound message.
As I heard from Rav
Matisyahu Solomon,
shlita, a yoke is simply
a tool that allows the
animal to do its job in
the most efficient and
easy way possible. How
else could it pull the
wagon if it did not have
a yoke? So, too, when
we proclaim the Shema
and publicly accept
‫עול מלכות שמים‬, we must
realize that the mitzvos
are perfectly crafted,
precise tools that allow
us to do OUR job here
on earth! How would we
manage without them?
Instant Insight:
The letters of the word
‫ע‬-‫מ‬-‫ ש‬are the same as
the letters that begin
the words ‫עול מלכות‬
‫ שמים‬but backwards!
The message is that
even when Torah
laws and values
seem “backwards” to
us — as if they make no
sense — nevertheless, we
accept that in truth they
are the tools for our
success.
Instant Insight:
In the Torah, the
pasuk of the Shema
has the letters ‫ע׳‬
and ‫( ד׳‬which spell
‫ — עד‬witness) enlarged.
The very miracle of our
survival — that after all
these years Jews all over
the world are united
in their proclamation
of the Shema — is itself
witness to the truth of
the Torah!

170

Important
Instant Insight:
“‫והסר שטן מלפנינו‬
‫ — ומאחרינו‬Remove
the Satan from
in front of us and
from behind us.”
The Satan “in front
of us” is clear:
it is the click on
the mouse we
shouldn’t make,
the outfit that’s
only appropriate if
we don’t breathe
or move, the
funny story about
someone we
shouldn’t say, and
the not-so-kosher
business deal we
should turn down.
But what is the
Satan “behind us”?
That is our past:
those mistakes
we made and our
insecurities that are
now whispering in
our ear, “Who do
you think you are
anyway? You’re
not so frum! That
next step of growth
isn’t for guys like
you — just give it
up!” All of this
is false, as our
mistakes in the
past do not prevent
our future growth!
We therefore ask
Hashem to help
us overcome the
“Satan of the past”
so we can continue
conquering new
levels of growth.

Some add the following before Half-Kaddish:
We Have Complete Faith That the Final Geulah Will Happen Soon
An Extra Brachah before We Start Shemoneh Esrei

Our Requests

‫שמנה עשרה‬
I end this day and prepare for the next by turning to
Hashem for His help.
Opening Statement

:‫ש ָפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח ו ִּפי יַ ִ ּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶת ָך‬
ׂ ְ ,‫ֲאדֹנָ י‬
‫ּופי י ִַּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶתָך‬
ִ ‫ׂפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח‬
ָ ‫ ְש‬,‫( — ֲאדֹנָי‬I am standing here because I do
want to daven, create a relationship, and thank You for all the good
You do for me — which is sometimes hard to see or understand — and I
want to ask You for the help I need. But that does not come easy to me
for so many reasons. Therefore, I am asking, please) Hashem, open
my lips and help me express myself properly.
Section 1 — Praises

FAQ:
Why am I asking
Hashem for stuff?
Hashem knows
what I want, and He
also knows what
I really need! If I
really need it and
am supposed to
have it, He would
have given it to me!
FAQ:
Why should I
bother making
requests from
Hashem? I’ve asked
Him many times
before for stuff,
and He keeps on
saying no!
ANSWER:
Both very crucial
questions for a
serious Ben Torah
and Bas Yisrael!
See Appendix 1.

‫ גּוֹ ֵמל ֲח ָס ִדים‬,‫ֵאל ֶע ְליוֹ ן‬

Instant Insight:
The terms ‫עוזר‬, ‫מושיע‬,
and ‫ מגן‬represent
three levels of help
that we need in
our lives — from the
least to the most.
‫“ — עוזר‬help,” is
when we are on the
right track, doing
what we need to
do in the best way
possible, and we
ask Hashem to
please help us that
all should work out,
since nothing will
come to fruition
without His help.
‫“ — מושיע‬salvation,”
is when we are
completely at a
loss; we throw up
our hands and
say to Hashem,
“Save me, I have
no idea what to
do next!” The third
and highest level,
‫“ — מגן‬protect,” is
when we think
we’re on the right
track and think we
know what we’re
doing, but in truth
we are completely
off base! We ask
Hashem, “If I’m
going in the wrong
direction — ‫— מגן‬
protect me from
myself!”

all-powerful, You shower
us with acts of helpful
kindness (even though at
times it is hard to see that),
and everything is Yours.
When dealing with us, You
take into account the good
deeds of our ancestors,
and (everything You do for
us is in order to) bring Mashiach to us, their descendants, even if
we do not deserve it!

Instant Insight:
Why do we say
“listen to our voice
(‫ ?”)קולנו‬Shouldn’t
we ask Hashem to
listen to the words
we say? What we
are asking Hashem
is: “I may not be so
articulate and may
not know how to
say what I really
feel. But I hope You
can tell from the
sound of my voice
that I am doing
the best I can to
communicate with
You!”
Crucial
Instant Insight:
We asked Hashem
to “accept our
tefillos,” so what
are we adding by
requesting, “Do
not turn us away
empty-handed”?
Of course, we hope
that Hashem will
grant us what we
have requested,
but sometimes,
for reasons known
only to Him,
Hashem must say
no. We therefore
ask Hashem that if
the answer to our
tefillah must be a
no, then at least do
not send us away
feeling “emptyhanded” — with
a feeling of total
rejection. Let our
hearts be filled with
emunah that we
had the amazing
zechus and
opportunity to talk
one-on-one with
Hashem and He
heard our tefillah,
even if the best
thing for us right
now is a no.

Since tefillah was meant to take the place of korbanos, we add the
following epilogue:

ָ ֶ‫יְ ִהי ָרצוֹ ן ִמ ְּל ָפנ‬

ָ
Hashem , Who controls everything :‫ וְ ֵתן ֶח ְל ֵקנ ּו ְּבתוֹ ָר ֶתך‬,ּ‫ֵּבית ַה ִּמ ְקדָּ ׁש ִּב ְמ ֵה ָרה ְביָ ֵמינו‬
and has taken care of Bnei Yisrael :‫ימי עוֹ ָלם ו ְּכ ׁ ָשנִ ים ַק ְדמוֹ נִ יוֹ ת‬
ֵ ‫וְ ׁ ָשם נַ ֲע ָב ְד ָך ְּביִ ְר ָאה ִּכ‬
throughout our history , that the ‫ימי עוֹ ָלם‬
ֵ ‫ ִּכ‬,‫וְ ָע ְר ָבה ַליהֹוָ ה ִמנְ ַחת יְ הו ָּדה וִ ירו ׁ ָּש ָליִ ם‬
Beis Hamikdash be rebuilt quickly

in our days, and help us find

On regular weeknights, continue here with Full-Kaddish.
On Motzaei Shabbos, say Half-Kaddish and continue on page 189.

When he calls
out to Me, I will

‫ — בצרה‬I will be with
him in his pain.”
When you cry out
to Hashem when
you are suffering,
the person who is
sincerely close to
Hashem will realize
that His response
is: “‫ — עמו אנכי בצרה‬I
am right there with
you!” It is Hashem
Who gave you that
suffering for your
benefit! That is
truly the greatest
comfort!

As we transition from the kedushah of Shabbos to the non-kadosh workweek,
we proclaim that the Presence of Hashem is everywhere, and we ask that He
help us retain our kedushas Shabbos all week:

‫ְא ָּתה ָקדֹוׁש‬
ַ ‫ — ו‬We realize that You, :‫ש ָר ֵאל‬

Hashem, are completely beyond
,‫וְ ָק ָרא זֶ ה ֶאל זֶ ה וְ ָא ַמר‬
our comprehension, yet we sing
Your praises, just as the angels in Heaven proclaim:
‫ — ָקדֹוׁש ָקדֹוׁש ָקדֹוׁש‬The sanctity of
Hashem can be felt in ALL worlds,
from the very highest world of
angels down to the very lowest
world, our material world; ‫ְמֹלא‬
‫ — ָכל ָה ָא ֶרץ ְּכבֹודֹו‬His Presence can
be felt everywhere. And as the
Serafim (the highest levels of angels)
proclaim: ‫ — ָּברּוְך ְּכבֹוד ְיהֹוָה‬The
essence of Hashem is completely
beyond us (even the highest angels);
it is ‫ — ִמ ְּמקֹומֹו‬from His place (up
there, it all makes sense!).

‫בֹותינּו‬
ֵ ‫( — ֲא‬As we go out and face
the world we ask:) Hashem Who
took care of our ancestors, please
direct our hearts to Your service
and have mercy on us when we
make mistakes, for You, Hashem,
are forgiving and are kind to all
who call to You. Shower us with
Your blessings and save us from
difficulties. Happy is the person
who places their trust in You!

ֵ ‫ — ָּברּוְך הּוא ֱא‬Thank
You for creating us as Your unique
nation and giving us a Torah of
truth. Open our hearts to Your
Torah and inspire us to do the right
things! And may we live to see the
coming of Mashiach. ‫ֶבר ֲא ֶׁשר‬
ֶ ‫ָּברּוְך ַהּג‬
‫ — י ְִב ַטח‬Blessed is the person who truly
trusts in Hashem and is secure in his
belief that all Hashem does is for his
best (even if it is hard to see). Hashem
never abandons those who seek Him.

Full-Kaddish and Aleinu — page 185.

‫ספירת העומר‬
Counting Up to Kabbalas HaTorah
It’s important to think a bit before one does a mitzvah, so that one will be
doing it for the correct reasons and realize that he is fulfilling a mitzvah!
Therefore, some say the following before counting the Omer.

‫— ְל ֵׁשם יִחּוד‬I am prepared and ready
to fulfill the mitzvah of counting
the Omer as described in the Torah.
(And as Moshe told Bnei Yisrael upon

‫ָה ַר ֲח ָמן הוּא יַ ֲחזִ יר ָלנ ּו ֲעבוֹ ַדת‬
,‫ֵּבית ַה ִּמ ְק ָ ּד ׁש ִל ְמקוֹ ָמ ּה‬
:‫ִּב ְמ ֵה ָרה ְביָ ֵמינ ּו ָא ֵמן ֶס ָלה‬

The focus of this chapter is on the future, when the entire world will join with
Bnei Yisrael to sing praises to Hashem and submit to His Will. In addition, after
the introductory pasuk, this chapter has forty-nine words, corresponding to
the forty-nine days of the Omer!

‫עֹולם‬
‫‪ִ — You commanded us‬רּבֹונֹו ֶׁשל ָ‬
‫‪to count the Omer from Pesach to‬‬
‫‪Shavuos as an opportunity to reflect,‬‬
‫‪rethink our values, and step by step,‬‬
‫‪day by day, purify ourselves and thus‬‬
‫‪be prepared to accept the Torah again,‬‬
‫‪and our mission to the world, on‬‬
‫‪Shavuos. Hashem, in the merit of this‬‬
‫‪mitzvah of counting the Omer today,‬‬
‫‪may I be cleansed of my impurities,‬‬
‫‪and may I be able to connect to the‬‬
‫‪essence of my neshamah, which is‬‬
‫‪rooted in the Heavens above.‬‬

‫קריאת שמע על המטה‬
All’s Well That Ends Well!
The best way to merit being granted another day is by showing Hashem that
we have learned the lessons of this day and are planning to make tomorrow a
better one! The first place to start is the area of bein adam l’chaveiro — using
the gifts we’ve been given to make the world a more peaceful and unified
place. That won’t happen if our goal of waking up tomorrow morning is “to
get even”! Thus, for the good of our neshamah, we need to just “let it go,”
i.e., forgive those who have aggravated us and start a new day. As we know,
Hashem judges us the way we judge others. We want Hashem to “understand”
and give us another chance — so we must treat His children the same way. In
addition, this is good for our own emotional well-being and will help us have
a peaceful night’s sleep! As Rabbi Dr. Abraham Twerski, zt"l, says, bearing a
grudge (and having that heated conversation in your head with the person
who got under your skin) is like swallowing poison and hoping the other
person dies. You just make yourself a bitter and unhappy person! So, take a
deep breath and say the following slowly.

I should never again anger You or do things that are inappropriate. (Thus, please
make tomorrow the kind of day in which it will be easy to make the correct decisions.)

With Your great mercy, please wipe away the sins that I have committed, but please
not through suffering or illness. ‫ — י ְִהיּו ְל ָרצֹון ִא ְמ ֵרי ִפי‬May all that I have said and all
the thoughts in my heart be acceptable to You, Hashem, my rock and my redeemer.
You Control Our Sleep — and Waking Up!

‫תהילים ג‬
The focus of this chapter is that even though David HaMelech
is forced to flee from his own son who is mounting a rebellion
against him (talk about having a bad day!), nevertheless, his
trust in Hashem is strong and he has the ability to go to sleep,
trusting in Hashem that he will awaken to a better day!

198

Instant Insight:
“When he calls
out to Me, I will
answer him. ‫עמו אנכי‬
‫ — בצרה‬I will be with
him in his pain.”
When you cry out
to Hashem when
you are suffering,
the person who is
sincerely close to
Hashem will realize
that His response
is: “‫ — עמו אנכי בצרה‬I
am right there with
you”! It is Hashem
Who gave you that
suffering for your
benefit! That is
truly the greatest
comfort!

199 Krias Shema al Ha’mitah

Important
Instant Insight:
“‫והסר שטן מלפנינו‬
‫ — ומאחרינו‬Remove
the Satan from in
front of us and from
behind us.” The
Satan in front of
us is clear: it is the
click on the mouse
we shouldn’t make,
the outfit that’s
only appropriate if
we don’t breathe
or move, the
funny story about
someone we
shouldn’t say, and
the not-so-kosher
business deal we
should turn down.
But what is the
Satan behind us?
That is our past,
those mistakes
we made and our
insecurities that are
now whispering in
our ear, “Who do
you think you are
anyway? You’re not
so frum! That next
rung of growth
isn’t for guys like
you — just give it up!”
All of this is false, as
our mistakes in the
past do not prevent
our future growth!
We therefore ask
Hashem to help
us overcome the
“Satan of the past”
so we can continue
conquering new
levels of growth.

‫— ַה ְׁש ִּכיבֵנּו‬May we rest ‫ֱאל ֵֹהינ ּו‬
‫יבנ ּו יְ הֹוָ ה‬
ֵ ‫ַה ׁ ְש ִּכ‬
peacefully tonight and

Instant Insight:
As we go to
sleep, we ask
that Hashem
protect us from
all sides, similar
to Bnei Yisrael in
the desert, who
had “clouds of
glory” protecting
them from all
sides. Though our
understanding of
angels is limited,
we do know the
following: Michael
represents chessed
and mercy (he is
the angel who told
Avraham and Sarah
the good news that
they would have
a child), whereas
Gavriel represents
din, strict justice
(he overturned
Sodom). We do
want Hashem to
judge us so that
we can correct
our ways, but we
ask that “Gavriel/
din” should be on
the left (which
represents the
weaker, softer
side) and “Michael/
chessed” on
the right, the
stronger side.
Uriel represents
light and (Torah)
wisdom. Thus,
we ask that he
be in front of us
to lead us on the
right path. Rafael,
who represents
healing (he healed
Avraham after
his bris), should
be behind us to
“heal” us when
we stumble and
fall backward. But
these are “only”
angels. We really
want protection
directly from
Hashem Himself,
and ask that His
Presence should be
directly over our
heads, overseeing
all we do.

Mishkan as we traveled through the desert); all skilled in the battle
of Torah study, which protects us from the fear of the darkness
of our galus. Rashbam: (In praise of Bnei Yisrael:) Behold you,
Bnei Yisrael, sleep as secure as Shlomo HaMelech, who (like all
kings) needs sixty mighty skilled warriors with swords to protect
him from his enemies.

‫תהילים קכח‬
The focus of this chapter is that a Jewish home will be
tranquil, happy, peaceful, and productive only if it is based on
Torah values! As we look back at our day and go to sleep for
the night, we focus on what really matters — not our physical
“house” and possessions, but the values and spiritual content
of our “home,” for that is what brings ultimate shalom bayis.

‫— ִׁשיר ַה ַּמעֲלֹות ַא ְׁשרֵי‬True
contentment and happiness
is found among those who
fear Hashem and follow

The only thing to worry about are the choices you make, since everything else
is in the Hands of Hashem! If you quietly resolve (between you and Hashem),
as you fall asleep, that tomorrow you will do the best you can, then you have
nothing to fear!

for tomorrow are just between you and Hashem).
This is how we started our day — that we have “a unique and close relationship
with” the Adon Olam, which is why “I entrust my spirit into His Hand when … I
am awake.” And we end our day with the same feeling. Therefore, “I entrust
my spirit into His Hand when I sleep.”

‫נטילת לולב‬
It’s Sukkos! Time to Unite All Jews Like One to Serve Hashem
It is important to think a bit before you do a mitzvah so that you will be doing
it for the correct reasons! This is especially true with the mitzvah of lulav
and esrog, since it carries with it so many different symbolisms and has deep
Kabbalistic meaning. Therefore, some say the following Yehi Ratzon before
taking the lulav.

‫ללה‬

204

‫הלל‬
On Special Occasions, We Need to Say Special Praise and Thanks to Hashem
for Our Miraculous Survival!
We tend to forget that we were the first nation to have a publicly funded
orchestra! The Leviim, who were supported by maaser, sang and played
instruments in the Beis Hamikdash as part of the avodah. Hallel is the part of
tefillah that gives us an opportunity to fulfill “Ivdu es Hashem b’simchah”: get
in touch with the depth of our neshamah and sing out thanks to Hashem. I
know that ALL of us have struggles and difficulties. Some more than others.
But now is the time to focus on what we DO have, not what we are missing.
As a speaker (who has no shortage of struggles) once said: “If you focus on
the good and not on the bad, you will end up being as happy, if not more so,
than if you had done everything you wanted to do.” Read that line again and
then sing Hallel.

‫ ֱאל ֵֹהינ ּו ֶמ ֶל ְך‬,‫ָּברו ְּך ַא ָּתה יְ הֹוָ ה‬
,‫ ֲא ׁ ֶשר ִקדְּ ׁ ָשנ ּו ְּב ִמ ְצו ָֹתיו‬,‫ָהעוֹ ָלם‬
:‫וְ ִצוָ ּנ ּו ִל ְקרֹא ֶאת ַה ַה ֵּלל‬

‫— ָּברּוְך ַא ָּתה ְיהֹוָה‬You, Hashem, are the
Source of everything; ‫ֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫— ֱא‬You
take care of us; ‫עֹולם‬
ָ ‫— ֶמלְֶך ָה‬and
You rule the entire world; ‫ֲא ֶׁשר ִק ְּד ָֽׁשנּו‬
‫ֹותיו‬
ָ ‫— ְּב ִמ ְצ‬and through Your mitzvos
You elevated us; ‫— ו ְִצ ָוּנּו ִל ְקרֹא ֶאת ַה ַהּלֵל‬and You commanded us to recite the Hallel.

‫תהילים קיג‬
The focus of this chapter is to emphasize the difference between our concept
of God and that held by the rest of the world.

‫— ַה ְללּויָּה ַה ְללּו ַע ְבדֵי‬Let us, as loyal
servants of Hashem, praise Him
and all He does. From sunrise to
sunset, from the beginning of time
until eternity, all that Hashem
does is for the good, and should be
blessed. (Even though) He is high
and mighty and beyond the Heavens
(which is what the nations of the world
understand; we appreciate the fact that)
His Presence is also constant down
here on earth, where He takes care of
the most weak and defenseless and
lifts them up out of their troubles.
And therefore; ‫— ַה ְללּויָּה‬whatever
happens, praise Hashem!

‫ — ְּב ֵצאת‬When

very natural order of the world was

Omit the following on Rosh Chodesh, except for during Chanukah, and on the last six days of Pesach.

‫תהילים קטו‬
The focus of the first half of this chapter is that when the nations are victorious
over us, they attribute it to their false gods — which is a chillul Hashem.

‫ — ֹלא ָלנּו‬Give us life and prosperity,
but not so that we ourselves get
recognition or glory, but rather,
so that Your “Name” — Your power
and providence — will get the
recognition it is due! When we
suffer, the nations gloat saying,
“Where is their God?” We know the
truth: that You are all-powerful,
and their idols and the powers they
rely upon are powerless! And each
of us, with our own unique role;
‫ — ְּב ַטח ַּביהֹוָה‬trusts in You; and ‫ֶעזְ ָרם‬
‫ּומגִ ּנָם הּוא‬
ָ — You give each of us what
we need to fulfill our potential, and
You protect us from physical and
spiritual harm.

providence of Hashem is everywhere.

‫תהילים קטז‬
The focus of the first half of this chapter is that we have complete trust in
Hashem that He will always protect us.

‫תהילים קיח‬
The focus of the first four pesukim of this chapter is that all the nations will
join us in praising Hashem.
Each one of the following four pesukim is said out loud by the chazzan, and the congregation
responds by saying ‫ הודו לה׳ כי טוב כי לעולם חסדו‬as well as the next pasuk ahead of the chazzan.

: ֹ‫ הוֹ ד ּו ַליהֹוָ ה ִּכי טוֹ ב ִּכי ְלעוֹ ָלם ַח ְסדּ ו‬:‫חזן‬
: ֹ‫ הוֹ ד ּו ַליהֹוָ ה ִּכי טוֹ ב ִּכי ְלעוֹ ָלם ַח ְסדּ ו‬:‫קהל‬
: ֹ‫ש ָר ֵאל ִּכי ְלעוֹ ָלם ַח ְסדּ ו‬
ׂ ְ ִ‫ֹאמר נָ א י‬

is good; ‫עֹולם ַח ְסּדֹו‬

‫תהילים קיח‬
The focus of the next section of this chapter is Bnei Yisrael, at the end of days,
looking back and thanking Hashem for protecting them (as a nation, and each
Jew as an individual) through all the difficulties of galus.

‫אתי יָּה‬
ִ ‫ — ִמן ַה ֵּמ ַצר ָק ָֽר‬Through all my
difficulties, I called out to Hashem,
and He answered me and saved me.
There is no reason to be afraid, for
Hashem is always with me! It is clear
that it is better to rely on Hashem
than to trust in people or rulers.
Throughout history, the nations
surrounded me and attacked me
like a swarm of bees, but Hashem
drove them away. Though Hashem
has punished and rebuked me (for
my good), He never abandoned me,
and Bnei Yisrael live on and sing the
praises of Hashem. Let the gates of
the Beis Hamikdash open, and there
we will praise Hashem.

‫ ָענָ נִ י‬,‫אתי יָ ּה‬
ִ ‫ִמן ַה ֵּמ ַצר ָק ָר‬
,‫ יְ הֹוָ ה ִלי לֹא ִא ָירא‬:‫ַּב ֶּמ ְר ַחב יָ ּה‬
‫ יְ הֹוָ ה ִלי‬:‫שה ִלי ָא ָדם‬
ׂ ֶ ‫ַמה יַ ֲע‬
‫ טוֹ ב‬:‫ וַ ֲאנִ י ֶא ְר ֶאה ְבשׂ נְ ָאי‬,‫ְּבעוֹ זְ ָרי‬

‫ללה‬

‫תהילים קיח‬
The last four pesukim of this chapter describe Bnei Yisrael coming to the
rebuilt Beis Hamikdash to thank Hashem for the final geulah.
Each of the following four pesukim is recited twice.

:‫ ֵּב ַר ְכנו ֶּכם ִמ ֵּבית יְ הֹוָ ה‬,‫ָּברו ְּך ַה ָּבא ְּב ׁ ֵשם יְ הֹוָ ה‬
‫( — ָּברּוְך ַה ָּבא ְּב ֵׁשם ְיהֹוָה‬The Kohanim come to greet those who have come to bring korbanos
and say to them:) Blessed is the one who comes (to the Beis Hamikdash bringing
korbanos) in order to praise Hashem for all He has done. ‫נּוכם ִמ ֵּבית ְיהֹוָה‬
ֶ ‫ — ֵּב ַר ְכ‬We are
blessing you with the brachos that come from the kedushah of the Beis Hamikdash!

:‫ ַעד ַק ְרנוֹ ת ַה ִּמזְ ֵּב ַֽח‬,‫ ִא ְסר ּו ַחג ַּב ֲעב ִֹתים‬,ּ‫ֵאל יְ הֹוָ ה וַ ָ ּי ֶֽאר ָלֽנו‬
‫ֽאר ָֽלנּו‬
ֶ ‫( — ֵאל ְיהֹוָה ַו ָּי‬Those bringing the korbanos respond:) Hashem is our strength,
and He has lit up the darkness of our galus. Therefore, ‫ ַעד ַק ְרנֹות‬,‫ִא ְסרּו ַחג ַּב ֲעב ִֹתים‬
‫ — ַה ִּמזְ ֵּֽב ַח‬tie up my korban carefully (so it should not run away) until it is taken up to
the Mizbei’ach.

:‫ ֱאל ַֹהי ֲארוֹ ְמ ֶ ֽמ ָך‬,‫ֵא ִלי ַא ָּתה וְ אוֹ ֶ ֽד ָך‬

‫( — ֵא ִלי ַא ָּתה וְאֹו ֶֽדָך‬And those bringing the korbanos continue and proclaim:) You, Hashem,
were my constant strength (even though during the darkness of our galus we did not
always realize it!), and therefore I thank You; ‫רֹומ ֶֽמָך‬
ְ ‫ֹלהי ֲא‬
ַ ‫ — ֱא‬You are my (personal)
God and I praise You!

‫ — הֹודּו ַליהֹוָה ִּכי טֹוב‬Give thanks to Hashem, for all that He does is good; ‫עֹולם‬
ָ ‫ִּכי ְל‬

‫שדוח שארל ףסומ‬

212

‫מוסף לראש חודש‬
Another Month — Another Chance! Therefore, We Give
Extra Thanks!
Bnei Yisrael follow the lunar (moon) calendar and not the
solar (sun) calendar. Why? Because the moon seems to get
smaller and smaller and almost disappears; then suddenly,
just when you think the moon is gone, it reappears and
grows again. So too Bnei Yisrael. There have been many
times when the nations of the world assumed we were done,
finished, and would disappear, and then we pop up again
and grow bright and strong. The same is true with each of
us. We all have had times when we feel we are not growing;
we have become insignificant and are losing our “light.” But
we always have the ability to pop back, just like the moon
does every month. On Rosh Chodesh, extra korbanos of
Mussaf were brought, including the Korban Olah (literally,
“going up”), which symbolized our resolve to keep going up
in levels of our avodas Hashem, no matter how difficult. In
addition, the Chatas “sin-offering” was brought to help us
get a fresh start.
Unfortunately, now we do not have the Beis Hamikdash, but
in this tefillah, we will show Hashem that we are not happy
with our present state of galus, and that we wish for the
time when the Beis Hamikdash will be rebuilt and we will be
able to bring these korbanos.
As in every Shemoneh Esrei, we start with the three brachos
of praises of Hashem and end with the three brachos of our
thanks to Hashem. The special Rosh Chodesh Mussaf part is
in between those two sections.
Opening Statement

:ּ‫ִּכי ׁ ֵשם יְ הֹוָ ה ֶא ְק ָרא ָהב ּו ג ֶֹדל ֵלאל ֵֹהינו‬
‫אֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫ — ִּכי ֵׁשם ְיהֹוָה ֶא ְק ָרא ָהבּו גֹדֶל ֵל‬When I proclaim the providence
of Hashem (which I am now going to focus on in Mussaf), it will
become clear to all that everyone must ascribe greatness (and
therefore praise) to Hashem, our God.
:‫ש ָפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח ו ִּפי יַ ִ ּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶת ָך‬
ׂ ְ ,‫ֲאדֹנָ י‬
‫ּופי י ִַּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶתָך‬
ִ ‫ׂפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח‬
ָ ‫ ְש‬,‫( — ֲאדֹנָי‬I am standing here because I do
want to daven, create a relationship, and thank You for all the good
You do for me — which is sometimes hard to see or understand — and I
want to ask You for the help I need. But that does not come easy to me
for so many reasons. Therefore, I am asking, please) Hashem, open
my lips and help me express myself properly.

The terms ‫עוזר‬, ‫מושיע‬

In a minyan: When the chazzan says Modim, we say the following:

‫מודים דרבנן‬
When thanking Hashem, an “Amen” is not sufficient!

‫ַחנּו ָלְך‬
ְ ‫מֹודים ֲאנ‬
ִ — We thank You,
Hashem, for You are our God. You
control everything and have taken
care of Bnei Yisrael throughout our
history starting from the time of
the Avos. You are the Creator of all
humanity, of the entire universe,

‫ ָׁש ַא ָּתה הוּא יְ הֹוָ ה ֱאל ֵֹהינ ּו‬,‫מוֹ ִדים ֲאנַ ְחנ ּו ָל ְך‬
‫ יוֹ ְצ ֵרנ ּו‬,‫ ֱאל ֵֹהי ָכל ָּב ָׁשר‬,ּ‫וֵ אל ֵֹהי ֲאבוֹ ֵתינו‬
‫ ְּב ָרכוֹ ת וְ הוֹ ָדאוֹ ת ְל ִׁש ְמ ָך‬:‫אשית‬
ִׁ ‫יוֹ ֵצר ְּב ֵר‬
,ּ‫יתנ ּו וְ ִקיַ ּ ְמ ָּתנו‬
ָ ִ‫ ַעל ֶׁש ֶה ֱחי‬,‫ַה ָ ּגדוֹ ל וְ ַה ָּקדוֹ ׁש‬
‫ וְ ֶת ֱאסוֹ ף ָ ּג ֻל ּיוֹ ֵתינ ּו‬,ּ‫ֵּכן ְּת ַחיֵ ּ נ ּו ו ְּת ַקיְ ֵמנו‬

219 Mussaf for Rosh Chodesh

Instant Insight:
By all other brachos
of the Shemoneh
Esrei, we simply
respond “Amen”
to the chazzan’s
brachah. Why
here must we say
our own “Modim,”
as if an Amen is
not sufficient? All
other brachos are
requests. When you
are with someone
else and they
put in a request
for something
important, saying
simply, “And me
too — I also agree!” is
fine. He represents
you. That is Amen.
But if someone is
really bursting with
gratitude, especially
to Hashem, for
“our lives, which
are entrusted into
Your Hand” and
“for our miraculous
survival till today,”
then Amen is
impossible. No one
can “represent”
your inner feeling
of gratitude! It must
burst forth! Thus,

(Peace between one another:) ‫ָּב ְר ֵכנּו‬
‫ ֻּכ ָּלנּו ְּכ ֶא ָחד‬,‫ — ָא ִבינּו‬Our Father, bless all of us together to become one unified nation,
by allowing us to all equally see Your Presence (and thanks to such clarity, discord will
cease!). For You, Hashem, when we were in Your direct Divine Presence (at Har Sinai),
gave us a living Torah (which teaches us how to live together in harmony), and You also
gave us a love of doing kindness and righteousness. (This all leads to everyone sharing)
their blessings with each other, living with compassion, and thus having a full life
and living in peace (by forgiving others).
(Peace from war and danger:) ‫ — וְטֹוב ְּב ֵעינֶיָך ְל ָב ֵרְך ֶאת ַע ְּמָך‬And may it be favorable in Your
eyes to bless Your people, Bnei Yisrael, at all times and at every moment, with Your
peace (protection from wars and dangers).

‫מוסף לחול המועד‬
FAQ:
Really? Am I
supposed to
wake up on Chol
Hamoed and say to
myself: “I sooo wish
I could go bring an
animal sacrifice this
morning”? How are
we supposed to
relate to a concept
that seems so
foreign to us?
ANSWER:
Valid question! See
Appendix 9.

Special Days—with Opportunities to Focus on Specific Values
The Sefer Kuzari describes davening three times a day as an
opportunity to recharge our neshamah’s spiritual batteries
from its battles with all the very non-spiritual “junk” of the
material world. Even so, keeping our spiritual sensitivities
pure and our value system straight is not easy. We are
bombarded continuously with messages, values, goals, and
promises of instant happiness, which are all antithetical
to Torah and our mission to the world. Therefore, Hashem
gave us specific times in the year (Shabbos and the Shalosh
Regalim) to step back and reflect. Regarding the Yamim
Tovim, each focuses on a different value or pillar in our
unique status as Hashem’s chosen people. This is reflected
by the unique character of the korbanos brought on each
Yom Tov. (Here is not the place to go into the details of each
korban. The commentary of Rav Samson Raphael Hirsch
on the appropriate sections in the Chumash explains the
significance of each animal and offering.)
Unfortunately, now we do not have the Beis Hamikdash, but
in this tefillah we will show Hashem that we are not happy
with our present state of galus — and that we wish for the
time when the Beis Hamikdash will be rebuilt and we will be
able to bring these korbanos.
As in every Shemoneh Esrei, we start with the three brachos
of praises of Hashem and end with the three brachos of our
thanks to Hashem. The special Yom Tov Mussaf part is in
between those two sections.
Opening Statement

:ּ‫ִּכי ׁ ֵשם יְ הֹוָ ה ֶא ְק ָרא ָהב ּו ג ֶֹדל ֵלאל ֵֹהינו‬
‫אֹלהינּו‬
ֵ ‫ — ִּכי ֵׁשם ְיהֹוָה ֶא ְק ָרא ָהבּו גֹדֶל ֵל‬When I proclaim the providence
of Hashem (which I am now going to focus on in Mussaf), it will
become clear to all that everyone must ascribe greatness (and
therefore praise) to Hashem, our God.
:‫ש ָפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח ו ִּפי יַ ִ ּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶת ָך‬
ׂ ְ ,‫ֲאדֹנָ י‬
‫ּופי י ִַּגיד ְּת ִה ָּל ֶתָך‬
ִ ‫ׂפ ַתי ִּת ְפ ָּתח‬
ָ ‫ ְש‬,‫( — ֲאדֹנָי‬I am standing here because I do
want to daven, create a relationship, and thank You for all the good
You do for me — which is sometimes hard to see or understand — and I
want to ask You for the help I need. But that does not come easy to me
for so many reasons. Therefore, I am asking, please) Hashem, open
my lips and help me express myself properly.

Hashem took care of us from the beginning:

‫גבורות‬
Hashem is all-powerful:

‫עֹולם‬

,‫ַא ָּתה ִ ּגבּ וֹ ר ְלעוֹ ָלם‬
‫ ְמ ַחיֵ ּ ה ֵמ ִתים‬,‫ֲאדֹנָ י‬
:‫יע‬

Instant Insight:
The terms ‫עוזר‬, ‫מושיע‬,
and ‫ מגן‬represent
three levels of help
that we need in
our lives — from
the least to the
most. ‫“ — עוזר‬help,”
is when we are
on the right track,
doing what we
need to do in the
best way possible,
and we ask
Hashem to please
help us that all
should work out,
since nothing will
come to fruition
without His help.
‫“ — מושיע‬salvation,”
is when we are
completely at a
loss; we throw up
our hands and
say to Hashem,
“Save me, I have
no idea what to
do next!” The
third and highest
of ‫“ — מגן‬protect,”
is when we think
we’re on the right
track and think we
know what we’re
doing, but in truth
we are completely
off base! We
ask Hashem, “If
I’m going in the
wrong direction,
‫ — מגן‬protect me
from myself!”

:‫מוֹ ִריד ַה ָּטל‬

‫ ְמ ַחיֵ ּ ה‬,‫ְמ ַכ ְל ֵּכל ַחיִ ּ ים ְּב ֶח ֶסד‬

‫ישנֵ י‬
ֵׁ ‫ ו ְּמ ַקיֵ ּ ם ֱאמוּנָ תוֹ ִל‬,‫ֲאסו ִּרים‬
‫ ו ִּמי‬,‫ ִמי ָכמוֹ ָך ַּב ַעל ְ ּגבוּרוֹ ת‬:‫ָע ָפר‬

:‫יח יְ ׁשו ָּעה‬

‫ — ְמ ַכ ְל ֵּכל ַח ִיּים ְּב ֶח ֶסד‬You take care of us
with Your kindness; and with Your
great mercy You revive the dead
(literally Techiyas Hameisim, and You
also save those who are near physical
or spiritual death); You support
those who have fallen (physically
and spiritually); You heal the sick
(both physically and emotionally ill)
and free those in prison (whether
in real prisons or trapped in their own
sense of limitations); and You keep
the covenant You made with our ancestors. There is no other power like You at all;
You have total control over our lives and our ultimate salvation. (And that is why we
are turning to You and only You with the upcoming requests in our Shemoneh Esrei.)

:‫וְ נֶ ֱא ָמן ַא ָּתה ְל ַה ֲחיוֹ ת ֵמ ִתים‬
:‫ ְמ ַחיֵ ּ ה ַה ֵּמ ִתים‬,‫ָּברו ְּך ַא ָּתה יְ הֹוָ ה‬

Techiyas Hameisim. ‫ָּברּוְך ַא ָּתה‬
‫ — ְיהֹוָה‬You, Hashem, are the Source
of everything; ‫ — ְמ ַחיֵּה ַה ֵּמ ִתים‬Who performs Techiyas Hameisim. (Even though it has
not yet happened, we are so sure that it will that we say this brachah in the present tense!)

‫קדושה‬
Let us praise Hashem like the angels do!

Unlike angels who have a higher understanding of Hashem, we humans cannot
understand “Who” Hashem is — His very essence (represented by the word
“‫)”אותו‬. All we can perceive is what He does — referred to as “‫שמך‬.” Nevertheless,
in Kedushah we will praise Hashem like the angels do. Since we can only do
this when we are at the level of a tzibbur — a unified community — we therefore
only say this with a minyan.

‫עֹולם‬
ָ ‫ — נְ ַקּדֵׁש ֶאת ִׁש ְמָך ָּב‬Let us declare ‫שם‬
ֵ ׁ ‫ ְּכ‬,‫נְ ַקדֵּ ׁש ֶאת ׁ ִש ְמ ָך ָּבעוֹ ָלם‬
the kedushah of “‫ — ”שמך‬all that
‫ ַּכ ָּכתוּב‬,‫ישים אוֹ תוֹ ִּב ׁ ְש ֵמי ָמרוֹ ם‬
ִ ׁ ִּ‫ׁ ֶש ַּמ ְקד‬
Hashem does in this world; ‫ְּכ ֵׁשם‬
ָ
:‫ וְ ָק ָרא זֶ ה ֶאל זֶ ה וְ ָא ַמר‬,‫יאך‬
ֶ ‫ַעל יַ ד נְ ִב‬
‫יׁשים אֹותֹו ִּב ְׁש ֵמי ָמרֹום‬
ִ ‫ — ֶׁש ַּמ ְק ִּד‬just like
the angels declare the kedushah
of “‫ — ”אותו‬the essence of Hashem. ‫יאָך‬
ֶ ‫ — ַּכ ָּכתּוב ַעל יַד נְ ִב‬As the Navi tells us; ‫ְק ָרא‬

On Shavuos: this Yom Tov of
Shavuos, a time to focus on the privileges and responsibilities of having been given
the gift of Torah.
On Sukkos: this Yom Tov of Sukkos, a time to focus on being happy with the abundance
that Hashem has given each of us.
On Shemini Atzeres/Simchas Torah: this Yom Tov of Shemini Atzeres/Simchas Torah, a
time to focus on the joy of being Hashem’s chosen nation.
This entire day and the opportunities that it gives us are based on the fact that
Hashem chose us (on Shabbos add: with love) and took us out of Mitzrayim to give us
His Torah and Mitzvos.

‫קרבנות החג‬
The pesukim that detail the unique korbanos and nesachim
for each Yom Tov are now listed. As mentioned in the
introduction, each of the Regalim (Pesach, Sukkos, and
Shavuos) focuses on a different value or pillar in our unique
status as Hashem’s chosen people, and this is reflected by
the unique character of the korbanos brought on each Yom
Tov. (See the commentary of Rav Samson Raphael Hirsch
on the appropriate sections in the Chumash that explain
the significance of each animal and offering.) We read
these pesukim to show Hashem that we are not happy with
our present state of galus and that we wish for the time
when the Beis Hamikdash will be rebuilt and we will be able
to bring these korbanos.

FAQ:
Really? Am I
supposed to wake
up on Chol Hamoed
and say to myself:
“I sooo wish I
could go bring an
animal sacrifice this
morning in honor of
Pesach or Sukkos”?
How are we
supposed to relate
to a concept that
seems so foreign
to us?
ANSWER:
Valid question! See
Appendix 9.

‫ו ַּבח ֶֹד ׁש ָה ִר ׁ‬
‫אכת ֲעב ָֹדה לֹא ַת ֲעשׂ וּ‪:‬‬

‫‪Shabbos, they will be happy to‬‬
‫‪have You, Hashem, as their ruler. The nation that sanctifies the Seventh Day‬‬
‫‪(Bnei Yisrael, who make Shabbos a day of stepping back from day-to-day material‬‬
‫‪pursuits) gains the ability to be satisfied with whatever good You give them. You‬‬
‫‪made the Seventh Day unique; it is the most special of all days for it reminds us‬‬
‫‪that You are the Creator of all.‬‬

On all days continue here:

‫בֹותינּו‬
ֵ ‫ֵאֹלהי ֲא‬
ֵ ‫ֹלהינּו ו‬
ֵ ‫— ֱא‬Hashem, Who
controls everything and has taken
care of Bnei Yisrael throughout our
history, our merciful King, please
have mercy on us and respond to our
call! Though we are not deserving,
but in the merit of our Avos, please
let us live to see the rebuilding of the
Beis Hamikdash with joy. Restore
the avodah of the Kohanim and the
singing of the Leviim, and we should
all return to living in our Land. Then
we will be able to fulfill the mitzvah
of Aliyah LaRegel as the Torah
commands: that we should come to
appear before Hashem in the Beis
Hamikdash, each person bringing
donations and korbanos, based on
the abundance that Hashem has
given each person.

thank Hashem
